There isn't really anything I'm looking forward to.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or blaming yourself for things that have happened lately?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't think so. No. Like I said, I'm not really doing much of anything right now, so... there isn't really anything for me to mess up, I guess. So, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble concentrating, making decisions, or feeling like you're not thinking as clearly as you used to?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I guess so. Like... the other day, we had a shift-planning meeting at work. And I just... I couldn't hold the staffing numbers in my head. I couldn't even make a basic decision. Which is... um... it's terrifying, actually. Because that's normally exactly my job. So... yeah. I guess I'm not thinking clearly.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you're feeling down or sad most of the time, even when there's no specific reason for it?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I suppose so. It's just... lately, I wake up in my temporary apartment, and... I don't know. There's just this heavy, hollow feeling. It feels like the whole day is already ruined before I even drive to the plant. So... yeah. Most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight lately, like eating less than usual or losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know about my weight. I haven't been checking. But... my eating is just sort of... all over the place, I guess. Like, sometimes it'll get to be 4 p.m. and I realize I've only had coffee all day. But then... other nights, I'll just stand in the kitchen and eat takeout right out of the container. Even if I'm not actually hungry. So... yeah, it's changed.\n\nDoctor: Have you completely lost interest in doing things you used to care about, even things that used to matter a lot to you?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. I guess I don't really do much outside of work right now anyway. So... I don't know if I've lost interest. I just... I don't really do anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt completely unmotivated to do things that you used to care about, even things that were important to you, like work or hobbies?\n\nPatient: Um... I think I just said, I'm not really sure. I still go to the plant. I have to. So... I guess I'm motivated enough to do my job. I just... don't really have much else going on right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or feeling physically slowed down, like it takes a lot of effort to do even simple things?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess so. People at work have said something about it. Sometimes it just... takes a lot of effort to even pick up a binder or something. I'm just really slow. But then... other times, I can't sit still at all. I just pace around the production office, picking at my nails. Sitting just feels... unbearable. So... yeah, I guess it's both.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant loss of energy or feel fatigued most of the time, even when you haven't been doing anything physically or mentally taxing?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know. I guess I'm tired a lot. But... I work long hours at the plant, so... I think it's just normal tired. I haven't really noticed it being... out of the ordinary.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately, like having trouble falling asleep, waking up frequently during the night, or waking up much earlier than usual and not being able to go back to sleep?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess. I haven't really been sleeping well. I just... lie there for hours sometimes, replaying mistakes from work. Or... I'll wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning and I just can't get back to sleep. So... yeah. I just kind of drag through the day after that... like I never even rested.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a time when you weren’t working or had a day off—did you still feel just as tired, or did you have any energy to do things you might enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know. Even on my days off, I just... I don't really do anything. I guess I'm still just as tired. I don't really have the energy for... whatever else.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any days recently where you felt unusually upbeat, energized, or like you didn’t need much sleep?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe a little while ago. When this current assignment first started at the plant, I felt... I don't know, almost electrically upbeat for a bit. But... then I just got weirdly irritable. It just felt like... if anyone questioned my ideas, they were just slowing down something really obvious and... important. I don't know. It was just work stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually high, extremely confident, or so energized that you didn’t need much sleep, even if it was just for a short time?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know, failure just didn't even seem possible to me at the time. It was... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there was a time when you felt unusually upbeat, confident, or energized for at least several days in a row, where it was a noticeable change from your usual self and it happened most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know. I wasn't exactly keeping track of how many days it was. Maybe? It's hard to remember if it was... all day, every day. It just felt like a phase, I guess. I'm not really sure.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there was a time when you felt unusually high, extremely confident, or so energized that you didn’t need much sleep for at least a full week straight?\n\nPatient: Um... no. I don't think it was ever a full week straight. That seems... like a lot. Like I said, I wasn't really keeping track, but... no, probably not that long.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least four days where you felt extremely energetic, needed much less sleep than usual, and felt like you were on top of the world or unusually confident, even if others around you thought your behavior was out of the ordinary?\n\nPatient: I mean... maybe? I just remember being really focused on the workflow stuff. I don't know for sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
